## Where the Framing Breaks Down

**The series frames each article around an aircraft, and this article has none.** The sections on sizing, dependent systems and the flight test record therefore say little, and what carries the article is an argument about a register, an instruction and a handful of statements.

**The article's models break down in places it names.** The Poisson model of the allocation rate is a convenience that does not claim allocations are memoryless, and the article answers that limit by stating how much clustering would rescue the third reading, without assuming that there is none. The uniform null for the founding-year cluster gives a scale and cannot serve as a test, because the window was drawn after the cluster was seen. The ordinal bound for the X-76A is an upper bound only, since the [X-23][related_post_a320_martin_marietta_x23] is on the compiler's list of numbers never allocated \[[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]] while this series treated that aircraft at full length. The classification of out-of-sequence reasons was made by this article from the compiler's notes, and the compiler did not make it.

**Two limits concern the readings themselves.** The reserve reading is unsupported rather than refuted, because an unrecorded reservation leaves no record. **And the evidence that decides between the readings postdates the article's date of 14 December 2025**, so the answer given here was not available on that date. Epistemic State lists each statement that postdates it.

## The Source Base

**This article rests on a narrow base of primary documents and it says how narrow.** Everything about the allocations comes from one compiler's reconstruction, Andreas Parsch's, obtained from the Department under the Freedom of Information Act, read at its current version and at four archived versions. **Everything about the rule comes from one published instruction and two earlier editions**, the 2005 edition as the [X-67][related_post_a364_x67_slot_taken_by_xq67] article read it and the 1994 edition read directly here. Everything about motive comes from four press releases, one quoted statement, one set of released emails, and, for the X-50A precedent, one NASA monograph quoting the contractor's programme manager.

**What was read directly for this article**, rather than inherited, is the current register and its archived copies of 19 August 2025, 10 December 2025, 15 January 2026, 1 February 2026 and 12 February 2026, the missing-designations page at its current version and as archived on 12 November 2025, 12 February 2026, 20 February 2026 and 17 May 2026, DARPA's announcement, the contractor's release, the Army's release, the Air Force Reserve Command's announcement of the F-47, and the released public affairs emails in full. **A further ten documents were read directly as well.** They are the 1994 instruction's text, DARPA's announcement of the XRQ-73, the Great Horned Owl programme page, the semiquincentennial statute, the National Archives transcript of the Declaration, the National Security Act of 1947, the Army's history of 14 June 1775, the Air Force Reserve Command's announcement for the President's words, NASA's inventory of the X-vehicles read at its X-39 to X-50 entries, and Boeing's release on the X-50A. The registry records of thirteen research papers were read directly too, and three of them carry an abstract that the prose follows. **The instruction's clauses are quoted from the copy the [X-67][related_post_a364_x67_slot_taken_by_xq67] article read**, and the quotations match that text.

**The research survey holds 1,232 research records drawn from Crossref's registry of scholarly works, NASA's Technical Reports Server and the Defense Technical Information Center**, and thirteen of them are research primaries cited in the argument above. Of them, 22 are report primaries, being 1.8 percent, by the identifier rule set out under The Definition of Primary, 16 from NASA's Technical Reports Server and 6 from the Defense Technical Information Center. **The median publication year is 2017**, and 701 of the 1,169 dated records, being 60.0 percent, date from 2015 or later.

**The report-server share is small, as expected for a subject that is not engineering.** Those servers hold the X-plane programme literature and NASA's own inventory of the X-vehicles, while the fields this question sits in publish mostly elsewhere. **Two cited documents from the Defense Technical Information Center are not retrievable**, an edition of the designating instruction catalogued in 1997 and the Navy's 2012 report on naming vessels, because the registry's document server returns a refusal page. Both are cited as research works and neither informs a claim.

### The Definition of Primary

**Primacy is a property of the document, and this survey derives it from the identifier.** A record is a report primary when its address is on the NASA Technical Reports Server or it carries a digital object identifier under the 10.21236 prefix, which Crossref registers for Defense Technical Information Center reports. **Most of this article's primary evidence is not in the survey at all**, being the register, the instruction, the releases and the emails listed above, which are cited as references rather than as research records. **The rule does not reach the thirteen research primaries either**, which are journal papers and book chapters such as Ruggles and Brodie on wartime serial numbers and Azaryahu on street names, so 1.8 percent is a lower bound on how much of the survey is first-hand.

### Thin Subjects

**Five subjects that this article's argument depends on have thin literatures.** Each count below is the number of research records in this survey whose title carries at least one of the listed terms, with a hyphen read as a space. Where a row joins two groups of terms with the word together, a title must carry a term from each group.

| Subject | Title terms counted | Records |
|---|---|---:|
| a meaningful number in a designation or numbering system | designation, designations, design number, design numbers, numbering, numbering plan, serial number, serial numbers, version number, version numbers, identifier, identifiers together with commemoration, commemorative, commemorate, commemorating, anniversary, centennial, bicentennial, semiquincentennial, founding year | 0 |
| a passed, reserved or reused number | the numbering terms of the row above together with skip, skipped, skipping, reserved, reservation, reuse, reused, retired, retirement, reclaim, reclaimed, reassignment, recycling, recycled | 1 |
| estimation from serial numbers | German tank, serial number, serial numbers | 7 |
| a founding year as a chosen number | founding year, founding years, year of founding, foundation year | 0 |
| rules set against discretion | rules versus discretion, rule versus discretion, rules rather than discretion, rules and discretion, rules vs discretion | 6 |

**No title in the survey joins a designation or numbering term to a commemoration, so no study of commemorative design numbers in any designation system was found.** The nearest work concerns names rather than numbers. It includes the Navy's report to Congress on its policies and practices for naming vessels, in the heritage cluster \[[A Report on Policies 2012][research_departmentofthenavywashingtondc_2012]], and a study of commemoration practices in special operations organisations, in the commemoration cluster \[[Bineham 2013][research_bineham_2013]]. **The nearest by title is a study called Numerical Commemoration**, about collective remembrance in Israel \[[Zerubavel 2014][research_zerubavel_2014]]. Its title names the phenomenon studied here, and it has not been read for this survey, so it is cited for no more than its title says.

**The passed number has no literature.** The one title that joins a numbering term to one of these words concerns a designation system for recycled carbon fibres, where what is recycled is the material. Whether a passed number stays available, which is the question the section headed The Next Research Number Is a Test leaves open, is answered in the record only by the X-49 precedent.

**Serial-number estimation is a small and settled literature.** All 7 titles concern estimation from serial numbers. The estimator they share treats numbers as issued in sequence without choice, which is the assumption a chosen number breaks.

**No title names a founding year.** The founding-year class therefore rests on the three sponsors' own statements and on the register, and not on any study of the practice.

**The survey holds the opposition of rules and discretion thinly, and none of it concerns allocation.** Six titles set rules against discretion, one of them Kydland and Prescott's, in economic planning, capital regulation, teacher hiring, flexible regulation and family law, and none of them concerns the allocation of a number or a name. The 199 records of the discretion cluster study discretion as administrators exercise it, which is the other half of the instruction's pairing of a rule with a discretion to depart from it.

### What the Survey Admits

**The research survey admits a record only when a person reading its title finds it on this article's subject**, which is the set of fields named at the head of the section headed The Contemporary Literature. Many words these fields depend on also name something else, and a record that shares only such a word is excluded. Licence plates are chosen numbers when they are auctioned and something else in plate recognition and traffic policy. A symbolic number in numerical cognition is a numeral rather than a symbol, the German tank problem shares a word with real tanks, round numbers are also integers in school mathematics and smooth numbers in number theory, lucky numbers are also a sieve, a graph labelling and a protein, and a version number is also a field in a network protocol. Registration also names the alignment of images in guided surgery, an identifier is also a radiation instrument, and a namespace is also a feature of operating-system containers. Tributes to individual scientists and notices of the anniversaries of journals, societies, laboratories and academies are excluded as well, since they use a centennial only as a label. Correction notices, peer-review reports, meeting and festival notices, editorials introducing an issue or a collection, obituaries, reviews of books, exhibitions and recordings, dictionary, glossary and definition entries, encyclopedia entries of two pages or fewer, bibliography entries, and the figure and object records registered for parts of works are excluded too, because a survey counts research works and those are parts of works, reference aids or editorial events rather than works, and so is a second deposit of a document already counted, such as a preprint beside its journal version or one chapter on two publishers' platforms. **Several neighbouring literatures share this subject's words and not its question, and they are excluded as well.** Configuration management in general is excluded while the configuration identification of items is kept, numerical taxonomy applied to one group of organisms or products is excluded while its method is kept, and the psychology of military identity is excluded while military naming and tradition are kept. Naming in the sense of defining a phenomenon, such as naming violence or naming corruption, is excluded while the naming of places, people, parties and things is kept, and a review of a field or a history that an anniversary merely dates is excluded while the observance of the anniversary is kept.

**Some records of doubtful relevance are kept, 122 of the 1,232 in all.** They include personal naming traditions, kept as naming as a political act, exhibits and buildings of the 1876 exhibition, kept as records of that observance, the bicentennial programmes of professions and libraries, kept as observances of the national year, textbook chapters on the discrete uniform distribution, kept as the model the serial-number estimator assumes, and a report on a festival of persistent identifiers and the registration of plant cultivar names, kept as the practice of identifier registries. Titles reading only Lucky numbers or Number Thirteen, whose subject the title does not show, are among them, and a reader may weigh each accordingly. Auctions of licence plates are kept as well, as prices paid for chosen numbers.

**Every research title has been read for relevance, so the off-topic share that remains is a matter of reading judgement**, and no rate is claimed for it. A reading of titles cannot show that a work is relevant in substance, so this is a statement about titles only.

## Epistemic State

### The Article's Own Vantage Point

**The article is dated 14 December 2025 and written on 2 October 2026.** The allocations it is about happened inside its dateline. **Their publication and their explanation did not.** The register first showed both rows between 15 January and 1 February 2026, and DARPA's statement of the reason is dated 9 March 2026. The article writes from current knowledge and lists what postdates its date.

### Historical Fact, From Primary Documents

- The X-68A was allocated on 20 August 2025 and the X-76A on 20 October 2025, both to DARPA, and no research row carries any number from 69 to 75.
- The XRQ-72A, XRQ-73A and YMV-75A carry 72, 73 and 75 in other basic missions.
- The 2020 instruction refuses requests in reverse or skipped sequences and reserves to the allocating office the authority to skip at its discretion.
- DARPA stated that the X-76 designation is a deliberate nod to the revolutionary spirit of 1776.
- The Army stated that the 75 in MV-75 commemorates its founding year, 1775.
- The Air Force stated that the 47 has three meanings, and its public affairs office stated that General Allvin made the decision.
- The X-50A's number was asked for because of its meaning, by DARPA in the compiler's and NASA's accounts and by Boeing in its programme manager's, and the X-49 it passed was issued later, as NASA's 2003 monograph predicted.
- The Semiquincentennial Commission Act of 2016 fixes 4 July 2026 as the 250th anniversary of the founding, as marked by the Declaration of Independence.
- DARPA's announcement calls the XRQ-73 an X-plane.

### Measured From the Documents Rather Than Asserted by Them

- The pointer walk of 22 steps and its single advance of eight.
- The dates on which the X-68A and X-76A rows first appear in archived copies of the register.
- The insertion and removal of the compiler's sentence about the X-77, dated by archive capture rather than by the page's stamp.
- The 24 allocation events since November 2020 and the six that follow the rule as written, or seven if the RQ-170 row is set aside.
- The classification of the reasons given for 24 designations that are out of sequence or that caused a skip, 25 assignments in all, which is this article's reading of the compiler's notes.

### Engineering Analysis, Derived Here and Checkable

- The allocation rate, the Poisson tail of $1.83 \times 10^{-10}$, and the equivalence of seven numbers to 8.36 years of research allocation.
- The ordinal bound of 64 for the X-76A.
- The count of 23 two-digit numbers left above the research pointer.

### Inference, Stated as Such

- That the compiler removed his X-77 sentence because of DARPA's statement. The timing fits and the page does not say so.
- That the YMV-75A and X-76A respond to the 250th anniversaries of 1775 and 1776. Both were allocated in the run-up to them and the record states no common policy. The F-47A's stated meanings include a president's number as well as a founding year, so it joins the class by its year and not by an anniversary.
- That the X-76 was chosen as the F-47 was. Nothing in the record says who chose it.

### Statements in This Article That Postdate Its Own Date

- The register's publication of the X-68A and X-76A rows, between 15 January and 1 February 2026.
- The compiler's missing-designations versions stamped 3 January 2026, with and without the X-77 sentence.
- DARPA's and the contractor's releases of 9 March 2026.
- The compiler's revised note on the X-76A quoting DARPA.
- The observation that no X-69 or X-77 is public as of 2 October 2026.
- The register's copy last updated 23 August 2026, from which every row count is taken, with the counts of the version current on this article's date stated beside them where they differ.

### What the Record Does Not Settle

- **Who chose the number 76.** The instruction gives the skip to the allocating office and the statement gives the motive to nobody in particular. **The X-50's number was never settled on this point either**, its three accounts naming two different requesters.
- **Whether 69 through 75 can still be requested.** The instruction's text says no and the X-49 precedent says yes.
- **Whether the block was ever reserved.** No record of a reservation exists, and an unrecorded reservation would leave none.
- **Whether the founding-year class was a policy or a coincidence of three sponsors.** The record shows three statements and no directive.

## Out of Scope

- The X-76 as an aeroplane, which is the subject of the next article in this series.
- The F-47 and MV-75 as aircraft, which belong to no research series.
- The unmanned series' departures from sequence, which the [X-58][related_post_a355_x58_slot_taken_by_xq58] and [X-67][related_post_a364_x67_slot_taken_by_xq67] articles measured.

## Conclusion

**The leapfrogged block is seven skips made at once so that one number could be chosen.** The register shows a single allocation passing seven numbers, the series' rate gives seven unseen allocations in the gap a probability of about two in ten billion, the instruction provides no way to reserve a block, and the sponsor later said the number was a deliberate nod to 1776.

**What the anomaly reveals is that design numbers have started to carry messages.** Three of them in eleven months were chosen at least in part to match founding years. The rule the instruction states describes less than a third of the allocations made under it. **And the rule's refusal of skipped sequences, if it is applied as written, makes every chosen number permanent and every number it passes lost.** Whether it is applied as written will be known when the next research designation is issued, as either the X-69A or the X-77A.

## Equations

<!-- Equations generated by _lib/equations.py from _sources/equations/. Edit the data file and regenerate. Do not edit this section by hand. -->

This section collects the 38 display equations of the article in the order they appear,
each under its number and reproduced exactly as it stands in the text.
A title in bold names an established result,
and a title in italics beginning with the word Bespoke names a construction made for this analysis.
Of the 38, 19 are established and 19 are bespoke.
Design numbers are symbols and carry no unit, intervals between dates are in days unless they are marked in years, a rate counts new research allocations per year of 365.2425 days, and $h$ always means the highest research design number allocated at the time named, which the article reads as the instruction's last approved number.

**Equation 1, Block of Skipped Numbers and the Interval Between Allocations.**
The block is the seven consecutive research numbers 69 through 75, and the X-76A was allocated 61 days after the X-68A.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
\mathcal{B} \;=\; \{\,69, 70, \dots, 75\,\}, \qquad \lvert \mathcal{B} \rvert \;=\; 75 - 69 + 1 \;=\; 7, \qquad t_{76} - t_{68} \;=\; 61\ \text{days}
\tag{1}
$$

*Equation 2, Bespoke Pointer, Advance and Numbers Passed.*
The article tracks the highest research number allocated so far, its advance at each new allocation and the count of numbers that advance passes, a measure carried over from the X-67 article.
[X-67][related_post_a364_x67_slot_taken_by_xq67]

$$
h_k \;=\; \max\bigl(h_{k-1},\, n_k\bigr), \qquad \Delta_k \;=\; h_k - h_{k-1}, \qquad s_k \;=\; \max\bigl(0,\, \Delta_k - 1\bigr)
\tag{2}
$$

*Equation 3, Bespoke Total of Numbers Passed.*
Summing the article's count of numbers passed over the 22 steps gives four single gaps and one gap of seven, eleven in all.

$$
\sum_k s_k \;=\; 4 \cdot 1 \;+\; 1 \cdot 7 \;=\; 11
\tag{3}
$$

*Equation 4, Bespoke Comparison of the X-76A Step With Earlier Gaps.*
The seven numbers that the step allocating the X-76A passed exceed the four that every earlier step in the window passed together.

$$
\sum_{k < K} s_k \;=\; 4 \;<\; s_K \;=\; 7 ,
\tag{4}
$$

**Equation 5, Bounds on the Publication Lags.**
The archived copies of the register bracket the date each row first became public, which places it 32 to 49 days after the article's date and at least 87 and 148 days after the two allocations.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 15 January 2026][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2026_01]
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 1 February 2026][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2026_02]

$$
32 \;\le\; t_{\mathrm{pub}} - t_{\mathrm{d}} \;\le\; 49, \qquad 87 \;\le\; t_{\mathrm{pub}} - t_{76} \;\le\; 104, \qquad 148 \;\le\; t_{\mathrm{pub}} - t_{68} \;\le\; 165
\tag{5}
$$

*Equation 6, Bespoke Two Definitions of the Next Number.*
The article writes the next number as one above the highest allocation, which gives 77, and as one above the highest allocation made in sequence, the compiler's convention, which gives 69.
[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]

$$
\nu^{\mathrm{hi}} \;=\; 1 + \max \mathcal{A} \;=\; 77, \qquad \nu^{\mathrm{c}} \;=\; 1 + \max\bigl(\mathcal{A} \setminus \mathcal{C}\bigr) \;=\; 69
\tag{6}
$$

*Equation 7, Bespoke Forced Timing of In-Sequence Allocations in the Block.*
Under the reading that the block was allocated in sequence but not released, every number in it must have been allocated between the X-68A and the X-76A, because an earlier allocation would have put the X-68A below the pointer.
[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]

$$
n \in \mathcal{B} \ \text{allocated in sequence} \;\Longrightarrow\; t_{68} \;<\; t_n \;<\; t_{76} .
\tag{7}
$$

**Equation 8, Estimated Rate of New Research Allocations.**
The rate is the 22 new allocations after the first divided by the 26.289 years the register's research allocations span, which is 0.8368 a year.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
\hat{\lambda} \;=\; \frac{K - 1}{T} \;=\; \frac{22}{26.289\ \text{yr}} \;=\; 0.8368\ \text{yr}^{-1}
\tag{8}
$$

**Equation 9, Expected Allocations in the Window.**
The expected count of new allocations in the 61-day window is the estimated rate times the window, which is 0.1398.

$$
\hat{\lambda}\,\tau \;=\; 0.8368 \times \frac{61}{365.2425} \;=\; 0.1398 .
\tag{9}
$$

*Equation 10, Bespoke Poisson Probability of Seven Allocations in the Window.*
Treating new allocations as a Poisson process at the estimated rate, an assumption the article states is a convenience, the probability of seven or more in the window is $1.83 \times 10^{-10}$.

$$
\Pr\bigl[\,N \ge 7\,\bigr] \;=\; 1 - \sum_{i=0}^{6} e^{-\hat{\lambda}\tau}\,\frac{(\hat{\lambda}\tau)^{i}}{i!} \;=\; 1.83 \times 10^{-10} .
\tag{10}
$$

*Equation 11, Bespoke First-Term Approximation to the Tail.*
The Poisson tail is dominated by its first term, which lies within 1.8 percent of the full tail at the observed mean.

$$
\Pr\bigl[\,N \ge 7\,\bigr] \;\approx\; e^{-\mu}\,\frac{\mu^{7}}{7!} \;=\; 1.797 \times 10^{-10}, \qquad \frac{\Pr[N \ge 7]}{e^{-\mu}\mu^{7}/7!} \;=\; 1.018 ,
\tag{11}
$$

*Equation 12, Bespoke Scaling of the Tail With the Rate.*
Multiplying the rate by a factor scales the article's Poisson tail by about the seventh power of the factor times an exponential correction.

$$
\Pr\bigl[\,N \ge 7 \;\big|\; c\mu\,\bigr] \;\approx\; c^{7}\, e^{-(c-1)\mu}\, \Pr\bigl[\,N \ge 7 \;\big|\; \mu\,\bigr]
\tag{12}
$$

**Equation 13, Seven Numbers as Years of Research Allocation.**
At the estimated rate, seven new research allocations take 8.36 years.

$$
\frac{7}{\hat{\lambda}} \;=\; 8.36\ \text{years}
\tag{13}
$$

*Equation 14, Bespoke Rate Multiplier at a Five Percent Tail.*
Solving the article's Poisson model for the multiplier at which seven allocations in the window reach a probability of 0.05 gives 23.51, or about twenty new numbers a year.

$$
\Pr\bigl[\,N \ge 7 \;\big|\; c^{\star}\mu\,\bigr] \;=\; 0.05 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad c^{\star} \;=\; 23.51, \qquad c^{\star}\hat{\lambda} \;=\; 19.67\ \text{yr}^{-1} .
\tag{14}
$$

*Equation 15, Bespoke Distance of the Tail Below the Threshold.*
At the observed rate the article's Poisson tail lies 8.44 orders of magnitude below the conventional 0.05 threshold.

$$
\log_{10}\!\frac{0.05}{1.83 \times 10^{-10}} \;=\; 8.44
\tag{15}
$$

*Equation 16, Bespoke Reading of the Request and Discretion Clauses.*
The article reads the instruction as accepting a request only for one above the highest approved number, so that any allocation above that must be a discretionary skip by the allocating office.
[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]

$$
\text{request for } n \text{ accepted} \;\Longrightarrow\; n \;=\; h + 1, \qquad n \;>\; h + 1 \;\Longrightarrow\; \text{a discretionary skip by the office}
\tag{16}
$$

**Equation 17, Lag of the Sponsor's Statement.**
The sponsor's announcement of 9 March 2026 came 85 days after the article's date.
[DARPA, 9 March 2026][ref_darpa_x76]

$$
t_{\mathrm{DARPA}} - t_{\mathrm{d}} \;=\; 85\ \text{days} .
\tag{17}
$$

**Equation 18, Two-Digit Year Encoding.**
A design number between 1 and 99 can stand for a year only as the year's last two digits, which is the year modulo one hundred.
[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]

$$
n \;=\; y \bmod 100
\tag{18}
$$

**Equation 19, Publication Lag of an Allocation.**
The lag is the date an allocation became public less the date it was allocated, which is negative when the announcement came first.

$$
\delta t \;=\; t_{\text{public}} - t_{\text{allocated}}
\tag{19}
$$

**Equation 20, Numbers Passed by the YMV-75A.**
The vertical-takeoff series' highest number before the YMV-75A was 25, so the 75 passed the 49 numbers between them.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
s \;=\; 75 - 25 - 1 \;=\; 49
\tag{20}
$$

**Equation 21, Numbers Passed by the X-50A and the X-76A.**
The X-50A passed one number and the X-76A seven, the 49 was issued 464 days after the X-50A, and the two allocations are 23 years apart.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
s_{50} \;=\; 50 - 48 - 1 \;=\; 1, \qquad s_{76} \;=\; 76 - 68 - 1 \;=\; 7, \qquad t_{49} - t_{50} \;=\; 464\ \text{days}, \qquad 2025 - 2002 \;=\; 23\ \text{years}
\tag{21}
$$

**Equation 22, Tally of Reasons for Out-of-Sequence Numbers.**
The reason classes assigned to the register's 24 notes sum to 25, because the ES-14A's note gives two reasons.

$$
8 + 3 + 3 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 \;=\; 25 \;=\; 24 + 1
\tag{22}
$$

**Equation 23, Share of Departures With No Recorded Reason.**
Eight of the 24 notes recording a departure from sequence give no reason, which is one third.

$$
\frac{8}{24} \;=\; \frac{1}{3}
\tag{23}
$$

**Equation 24, Distribution of the Range of Uniform Points.**
For $J$ points placed independently and uniformly over a window, the probability that their range is at most a given fraction of the window follows from order statistics.

$$
\Pr\bigl[\,R \le w\,\bigr] \;=\; J\,x^{J-1} \;-\; (J-1)\,x^{J}, \qquad x \;=\; \frac{w}{W} .
\tag{24}
$$

*Equation 25, Bespoke Probability of the Founding-Year Cluster Under a Uniform Null.*
Under a uniform null over a window from 19 August 1998 to the end of November 2025 that the article drew after seeing the cluster, three allocations fall within 329 days with probability 0.00320, a scale rather than a test.

$$
\Pr\bigl[\,R \le 329\ \text{days}\,\bigr] \;=\; 3\,(0.03302)^{2} - 2\,(0.03302)^{3} \;=\; 0.00320 .
\tag{25}
$$

**Equation 26, Share of Allocations Following the Rule as Written.**
Six of the 23 allocation events since November 2020 that had a predecessor took one above the highest number in their basic mission, which is 0.261.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
f \;=\; \frac{6}{23} \;=\; 0.261
\tag{26}
$$

*Equation 27, Bespoke Share With the RQ-170 Row Set Aside.*
Setting aside the RQ-170 row, whose official form the compiler leaves uncertain, makes the XRQ-73A one above the unmanned maximum and raises the share to seven of 23.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
f' \;=\; \frac{7}{23} \;=\; 0.304
\tag{27}
$$

**Equation 28, Share Following the Rule in the Research Series.**
The research series took the next number at all four of its events from 2021 to 2023 and at neither of its two events in 2025.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
f_X^{\,\text{2021 to 2023}} \;=\; \frac{4}{4} \;=\; 1, \qquad f_X^{\,\text{2025}} \;=\; \frac{0}{2} \;=\; 0 .
\tag{28}
$$

*Equation 29, Bespoke Ordinal Position of a Design Number.*
Reading the design number as an ordinal, the article defines the ordinal position as the design number less the count of never-allocated numbers below it.
[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]

$$
o(n) \;=\; n \;-\; \bigl|\, G \cap [1, n) \,\bigr|
\tag{29}
$$

*Equation 30, Bespoke Ordinal Position of the X-76A.*
Assuming that every number below 76 absent from the compiler's list was allocated, the X-76A is at most the sixty-fourth research allocation and its number overstates its place by 18.75 percent.
[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]

$$
o(76) \;=\; 76 - 12 \;=\; 64, \qquad \frac{76}{64} - 1 \;=\; 0.1875 .
\tag{30}
$$

*Equation 31, Bespoke Overstatement Before and After the X-76A.*
Under the same assumption the X-68A was at most the sixty-third allocation, and the share of printed numbers that stand for no aircraft rose from 0.0735 to 0.158.
[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]

$$
o(68) \;=\; 68 - 5 \;=\; 63, \qquad \frac{5}{68} \;=\; 0.0735 \;\;\longrightarrow\;\; \frac{12}{76} \;=\; 0.158
\tag{31}
$$

*Equation 32, Bespoke Condition for Encoding a Year Under the 2020 Rule.*
Reading the 2020 rule literally, a year can be encoded in two digits only if its last two digits exceed the current pointer.
[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]

$$
y \bmod 100 \;>\; h_t ,
\tag{32}
$$

**Equation 33, Two-Digit Numbers Above the Research Pointer.**
With the research pointer at 76, the two-digit numbers still above it are the 23 from 77 through 99.

$$
99 - 76 \;=\; 23
\tag{33}
$$

**Equation 34, Share of a Century Still Encodable.**
Those 23 values are 0.23 of the years in any century.

$$
\frac{99 - 76}{100} \;=\; 0.23
\tag{34}
$$

*Equation 35, Bespoke Overcount of an Observer Reading the Number as an Ordinal.*
An observer who read the X-76A as the seventy-sixth research aircraft would overcount by the twelve numbers that the article's ordinal position removes.

$$
76 - o(76) \;=\; 12 .
\tag{35}
$$

**Equation 36, Minimum-Variance Unbiased Serial Number Estimator.**
The classical estimate of the total issued from $\kappa$ distinct serial numbers with largest $m$ is $m$ times one plus the reciprocal of $\kappa$, less one.
[Anthony Lee and Steven Miller 2023][research_anthonylee_stevenmiller_2023]
[Ruggles and Brodie 1947][research_ruggles_brodie_1947]

$$
\hat{M} \;=\; m\Bigl(1 + \frac{1}{\kappa}\Bigr) - 1 .
\tag{36}
$$

*Equation 37, Bespoke Next Numbers in the Unmanned Series.*
Applied to the unmanned series, the article's two definitions give 29 and 30 for the compiler's convention, which the RQ-29A and YRQ-30A took, against 171 for one above the highest allocation.
[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]

$$
n_{\text{RQ-29A}} \;=\; 29 \;=\; \nu^{\mathrm{c}}, \qquad n_{\text{YRQ-30A}} \;=\; 30 \;=\; \nu^{\mathrm{c}}, \qquad \nu^{\mathrm{hi}} \;=\; 171 ,
\tag{37}
$$

**Equation 38, Shares of Commemorative Naming Titles.**
Of the 186 records in the commemorative naming cluster, 51 titles name an anniversary, commemoration or memory, 32 name renaming and 24 name a regime or political transition.

$$
\frac{51}{186} \;=\; 0.274, \qquad \frac{32}{186} \;=\; 0.172, \qquad \frac{24}{186} \;=\; 0.129 .
\tag{38}
$$

### Notation

- $\mathcal{B}$, the block of research numbers 69 through 75
- $t_{68}$, the allocation date of the X-68A, with $t_{76}$, $t_{49}$ and $t_{50}$ those of the X-76A, the X-49A and the X-50A
- $t_n$, the allocation date of design number $n$
- $t_{\mathrm{pub}}$, the first appearance of a row in an archived copy of the register
- $t_{\mathrm{d}}$, this article's editorial date of 14 December 2025
- $t_{\mathrm{DARPA}}$, the announcement by the Defense Advanced Research Projects Agency of 9 March 2026
- $n_k$, the design number of the $k$th new research allocation in date order
- $h_k$, the highest research design number allocated up to allocation $k$, with $h_t$ the highest before time $t$
- $\Delta_k$, the advance of that highest number at allocation $k$
- $s_k$, the numbers passed at allocation $k$, with $s_{50}$ and $s_{76}$ those passed by the X-50A and the X-76A
- $K$, the number of new research allocations in the register, and the step that allocated the X-76A
- $\mathcal{A}$, the set of research design numbers allocated
- $\mathcal{C}$, the subset of $\mathcal{A}$ judged chosen out of sequence
- $\nu^{\mathrm{hi}}$, the next number as one above the highest allocated
- $\nu^{\mathrm{c}}$, the next number as one above the highest allocated in sequence
- $T$, the span of the research allocations, in years
- $\hat{\lambda}$, the estimated rate of new research allocations, per year
- $\tau$, the window between the X-68A and the X-76A, in days
- $\mu$, the expected allocations in that window, $\hat{\lambda}\tau$
- $N$, the number of allocations in that window under the Poisson model
- $c^{\star}$, the multiplier on the rate at which the tail reaches 0.05, with $c$ a general multiplier
- $y$, a calendar year
- $\delta t$, the public announcement date minus the allocation date, in days
- $J$, the number of founding-year allocations
- $R$, the range of their allocation dates
- $W$, the register's window, with $w$ the observed span of the founding-year class and $x$ the ratio of the two
- $f_X$, the fraction of allocation events following the rule as written within the research series, with $f$ the fraction across the register
- $G$, the set of research design numbers never allocated
- $o(n)$, the ordinal position of design number $n$ among those actually allocated
- $\kappa$, the number of distinct serial numbers an observer has seen
- $\hat{M}$, the estimate of the total number issued, from the largest serial number seen $m$
